**Previous review covered**:
- (a) Decision rationality — all info level
- (b) Page compression — warning about free-form ablation to Appendix


- (c) RE multi-seed risk — info, manageable
- (d) LP T=0.7 — info, worth running

**New trigger asks specifically**:
1. RE multi-seed recurring critical risk
2. Page compression info loss (Discussion ablation → Appendix / Table 2 N=16 → Appendix / WNUT-17)
3. Standing with R1 vs R2's fundamental reframing

There's a discrepancy I'm noticing: the new strategy says "不补跑 LP T=0.7" — but earlier D087 indicated they were actually running LP T=0.7 (exp_017_llama_conll_n8_), so I need to clarify whether this is a change in plan or a misstatement.

Looking at the new trigger more carefully, it says "R7-W1（LP T=0.7）：Limitations 讨论 + 引用 free-form std ratio 0.97。不补跑。" — meaning they're choosing to discuss LP T=0.7 in the Limitations section rather than run it as a new experiment. But exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 was already running on GPU, so I need to reconcile whether that existing run is still happening or if the strategy has shifted to skip it entirely. I'm noticing a potential timing issue—exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 is actually running right now with high GPU utilization, so either the strategy was drafted before launch or "不补跑" means no *additional* T=0.7 experiments beyond this one. I should flag this inconsistency.

On the R2 concern about the tautological gap: R2 is pushing back on whether the Layer 1 argument (that 4/5 signals are instance-level aggregates and thus can't rank candidates) is even an empirical contribution or just a definitional observation. The current strategy leans toward presentation improvements rather than fundamental reframing, which aligns with R1's borderline accept stance.

The key insight is that if the paper cleanly separates Layer 1 (the definitional limitation) from Layer 2 (the empirical finding that even LP fails due to compression), then R2's tautological concern gets addressed without needing to overhaul the argument. Downgrading C1 to a "finding" and restructuring Table 3 should make this distinction clear enough that R1's perspective holds.

There's still a gamble here—if R2 is the more influential reviewer and presentation tweaks don't push them above 2.5, the strategy relies on the AC either favoring R1's view or accepting that the empirical contribution is solid enough. The "8B-Scale" qualifier in the title is honest about scope limitations, which works for an 8-page Findings paper.

Now I'm ready to draft the review response, focusing on those three specific questions and any critical gaps. I should pull in what the Director flagged as the main issues rather than re-reading the full review file.

For the multi-seed RE concern, I can point to the two existing seeds (123 and 42) that show convergence on the key metrics—SJ ρ stays around 0.28 and zero-F1 hovers near 44%—so there's actually 2-seed coverage for N=16. The paper just needs to be consistent about calling RE results "preliminary" and not overclaiming. If R2 pushes back on why RE doesn't match NER's 3-seed rigor, the answer is straightforward: RE performance is so consistently weak that extra seeds don't add much signal.

On page compression, the plan to move the free-form ablation to the appendix should free up space, and I need to verify that this trade-off actually lands the paper within limits without sacrificing clarity.

For the reviewer dynamics, R1 is borderline accept at 3.5 while R2 is borderline reject at 2.5, so I need to address both sets of concerns even though they're pulling in different directions.

My strategy is to tackle R2's concrete issues—page limits, statistical rigor, and evidence for the RE layer—while making presentation tweaks on the framing disagreement rather than doing a fundamental restructure. A major rewrite in 10 days risks breaking the narrative R1 already bought into, so I'm betting the AC will see that we've genuinely engaged with the feedback if the improvements are substantive enough.

One thing I need to clarify though: the strategy notes "不补跑" for the LP T=0.7 experiment, but exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 is currently running, so I should check what's actually happening there before finalizing.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"import json\\nimport csv\\nimport sys\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code\\\")\\nfrom evaluation import per_instance_f1, entity_strict_match, _prf\\n\\nDATA_PATH = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/exp_012_rerun_1024/samples.jsonl\\\"\\nOUT_CSV = \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/analysis_round8/epsilon_sweep.csv\\\"\\n\\nEPSILONS = [0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20]\\nN_BOOT = 10000\\nRNG = np.random.RandomState(42)\\n\\n# Load data, filter gold-empty\\ninstances = []\\nwith open(DATA_PATH) as f:\\n    for line in f:\\n        inst = json.loads(line)\\n        if len(inst[\\\"gold\\\"].get(\\\"entities\\\", [])) == 0:\\n            continue\\n        instances.append(inst)\\n\\nprint(f\\\"Loaded {len(instances)} instances (after gold-empty filter)\\\")\\n\\n# Verify N=8\\nn_samples_check = [len(inst[\\\"samples\\\"]) for inst in instances]\\nassert all(n == 8 for n in n_samples_check), f\\\"Not all instances have 8 samples: {set(n_samples_check)}\\\"\\n\\n# Precompute per-instance values\\nlp_ranges = []\\ngreedy_f1s = []\\nlp_best_f1s = []\\nsample_f1s_per_inst = []\\n\\nfor inst in instances:\\n    gold = inst[\\\"gold\\\"]\\n    samples = inst[\\\"samples\\\"]\\n    \\n    # LP range: max - min of mean_logprob across 8 samples\\n    mean_lps = [s[\\\"mean_logprob\\\"] for s in samples]\\n    lp_range = max(mean_lps) - min(mean_lps)\\n    lp_ranges.append(lp_range)\\n    \\n    # Greedy F1\\n    greedy = inst[\\\"greedy\\\"]\\n    g_f1 = per_instance_f1(greedy, gold, \\\"ner\\\")\\n    greedy_f1s.append(g_f1)\\n    \\n    # LP best-of-N: select sample with highest mean_logprob\\n    best_idx = int(np.argmax(mean_lps))\\n    lp_best = per_instance_f1(samples[best_idx], gold, \\\"ner\\\")\\n    lp_best_f1s.append(lp_best)\\n    \\n    # All sample F1s (for reference)\\n    sample_f1s_per_inst.append([per_instance_f1(s, gold, \\\"ner\\\") for s in samples])\\n\\nlp_ranges = np.array(lp_ranges)\\ngreedy_f1s = np.array(greedy_f1s)\\nlp_best_f1s = np.array(lp_best_f1s)\\n\\nprint(f\\\"LP range stats: min={lp_ranges.min():.6f}, median={np.median(lp_ranges):.6f}, max={lp_ranges.max():.6f}\\\")\\n\\n# Bootstrap paired test\\ndef bootstrap_paired_test(a, b, n_boot=N_BOOT, rng=RNG):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Test if mean(a) > mean(b). Returns p-value (one-sided).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    diff = a - b\\n    observed = diff.mean()\\n    n = len(diff)\\n    count_le_zero = 0\\n    for _ in range(n_boot):\\n        idx = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\\n        boot_diff = diff[idx].mean()\\n        if boot_diff <= 0:\\n            count_le_zero += 1\\n    return count_le_zero / n_boot\\n\\n# Sweep\\nresults = []\\nfor eps in EPSILONS:\\n    tied_mask = lp_ranges < eps\\n    non_tied_mask = ~tied_mask\\n    \\n    tied_frac = tied_mask.sum() / len(instances)\\n    non_tied_n = non_tied_mask.sum()\\n    \\n    if non_tied_n == 0:\\n        results.append({\\n            \\\"epsilon\\\": eps,\\n            \\\"tied_fraction\\\": tied_frac,\\n            \\\"non_tied_n\\\": 0,\\n            \\\"non_tied_selection_delta_pp\\\": float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n            \\\"non_tied_p\\\": float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n            \\\"non_tied_mean_f1\\\": float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n            \\\"greedy_mean_f1_on_non_tied\\\": float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n        })\\n        continue\\n    \\n    nt_lp_best = lp_best_f1s[non_tied_mask]\\n    nt_greedy = greedy_f1s[non_tied_mask]\\n    \\n    delta_pp = (nt_lp_best.mean() - nt_greedy.mean()) * 100\\n    p_val = bootstrap_paired_test(nt_lp_best, nt_greedy)\\n    \\n    results.append({\\n        \\\"epsilon\\\": eps,\\n        \\\"tied_fraction\\\": round(tied_frac, 4),\\n        \\\"non_tied_n\\\": int(non_tied_n),\\n        \\\"non_tied_selection_delta_pp\\\": round(delta_pp, 2),\\n        \\\"non_tied_p\\\": round(p_val, 4),\\n        \\\"non_tied_mean_f1\\\": round(nt_lp_best.mean() * 100, 2),\\n        \\\"greedy_mean_f1_on_non_tied\\\": round(nt_greedy.mean() * 100, 2),\\n    })\\n    \\n    print(f\\\"ε={eps:.2f}: tied={tied_frac:.1%}, non_tied_n={non_tied_n}, \\\"\\n          f\\\"Δ={delta_pp:+.2f}pp, p={p_val:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n# Write CSV\\nwith open(OUT_CSV, \\\"w\\\", newline=\\\"\\\") as f:\\n    writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=[\\n        \\\"epsilon\\\", \\\"tied_fraction\\\", \\\"non_tied_n\\\",\\n        \\\"non_tied_selection_delta_pp\\\", \\\"non_tied_p\\\",\\n        \\\"non_tied_mean_f1\\\", \\\"greedy_mean_f1_on_non_tied\\\"\\n    ])\\n    writer.writeheader()\\n    writer.writerows(results)\\n\\nprint(f\\\"\\\\nCSV saved to {OUT_CSV}\\\")\\n\\n# Also print table\\nprint(\\\"\\\\n\\\" + \\\"=\\\"*90)\\nprint(f\\\"{'ε':>6} {'tied%':>7} {'non_tied_n':>10} {'Δ(pp)':>8} {'p':>8} {'LP_F1':>8} {'greedy_F1':>10}\\\")\\nprint(\\\"-\\\"*90)\\nfor r in results:\\n    print(f\\\"{r['epsilon']:>6.2f} {r['tied_fraction']:>7.1%} {r['non_tied_n']:>10} \\\"\\n          f\\\"{r['non_tied_selection_delta_pp']:>8.2f} {r['non_tied_p']:>8.4f} \\\"\\n          f\\\"{r['non_tied_mean_f1']:>8.2f} {r['greedy_mean_f1_on_non_tied']:>10.2f}\\\")\\nprint(\\\"=\\\"*90)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.35}}"}

```text
Now I understand what the worker did. It ran an epsilon sweep analysis — computing tied fraction, non-tied selection delta, and bootstrap p-values for different epsilon thresholds. This is one of the 4 CPU analysis tasks mentioned in D088.

The results are interesting:
- ε=0.01: tied=1.1%, n=523, Δ=+0.58pp, p=0.21 (not significant)
- ε=0.02: tied=13.6%, n=457, Δ=+0.83pp, p=0.15


- ε=0.03: tied=31.8%, n=361, Δ=+1.31pp, p=0.08
- ε=0.05: tied=60.3%, n=210, Δ=+2.86pp, p=0.004** (significant!)
- ε=0.10: tied=83.9%, n=85, Δ=+3.69pp, p=0.034*
- ε=0.15: tied=93.2%, n=36, Δ=+4.15pp, p=0.11
- ε=0.20: tied=96.4%, n=19, Δ=+4.59pp, p=0.13

There's a clear pattern emerging: as epsilon increases, more pairs get classified as tied, the sample size shrinks, but the selection delta grows substantially. The sweet spot appears to be around ε=0.05 where we get statistical significance with a meaningful effect size. The p-value discrepancy is interesting — the CSV shows p=0.0035 at ε=0.05 with 10000 bootstrap iterations, whereas paper_framing reported p=0.01, likely due to different bootstrap implementations or sample sizes. But the key point holds: the non-tied subset at ε=0.05 matches the paper's reported metrics (39.7% non-tied, +2.86pp effect), so this is the same experiment with just a more precise p-value calculation.

Now I'm auditing the code quality and data consistency. The epsilon_sweep.py script looks solid — it's pulling from the correct unified 1024-token pipeline, properly filtering gold-empty instances, asserting N=8, and running a robust bootstrap test with 10000 iterations and a fixed seed. I need to verify that the "60% within-sample range < 0.05" claim from paper_framing aligns with what the epsilon sweep is actually measuring.

The data checks reveal mostly alignment: the sweep confirms 60.3% tied fraction at ε=0.05, the performance gain matches at +2.86pp versus greedy, and the 39.7% non-tied percentage is essentially 40%. However, there's a p-value discrepancy — the paper reports p=0.01 while the sweep shows p=0.0035, which is actually more significant and in a favorable direction. I should flag this for reconciliation, either updating the paper to reflect the more precise p-value or documenting why the difference exists. The sweep data also looks promising for populating an appendix table showing how the epsilon threshold affects the performance delta, though I need to verify the monotonic relationship holds across the range.

Now I'm checking the GPU allocation — cuda:0 is running at 81% with one experiment, and cuda:1 appears to have a job assigned but the directory is empty, which suggests it might not have actually started. Let me refresh the status to see the current state.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
GPU white list is cuda:0, cuda:1, cuda:2, cuda:3 — all four GPUs. So both processes are within the allowed range. No GPU boundary violation.

Now let me compose the final review. The key findings:

1. **GPU check**: Both vLLM processes on cuda:0 and cuda:1 are within project gpu_ids [0,1,2,3]. No violation. But there are TWO processes running while registry only shows ONE experiment (exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07). The second process is unregistered.


2. **Strategy discrepancy**: The strategy document says "R7-W1（LP T=0.7）：不补跑" but exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 is actually a T=0.7 experiment that's currently running. This suggests either the strategy was written before this experiment launched, or the Director made a new decision to proceed with it. Looking back at the trigger message, it mentions "R7-W1（LP T=0.7）：Limitations 讨论 + 引用 free-form std ratio" which seems to indicate a different context than the original strategy note.

The experiment was created on 2026-05-15 at 15:29:34, and D087 was made just minutes later at 15:32 UTC, so D087 came after the experiment was already launched. The "不补跑" instruction might mean no *additional* experiments beyond what's already running. The real question is whether the new Director intends to use results from exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 or just discuss T=0.7 theoretically in the Limitations section.

I'm also noticing there are two vLLM processes running — one for exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07, but what's the other one? It could be leftover from the Selection F1 Table 2 analysis worker that already finished, or a new worker just started. Either way, an unregistered GPU process is worth flagging.

Now focusing on the three core questions for the review. For the RE multi-seed concern: two seeds (42 and 123) show converging results, the paper marks RE as preliminary, and it's a diagnostic claim rather than a core one. A reviewer might push for three seeds like NER, but the preliminary framing plus convergence across two seeds should provide adequate defense.

On page compression and information loss: the free-form ablation is the critical risk since it's the sole evidence for universality, but if a condensed summary stays in the main text, that works. N-scaling in the appendix is secondary evidence and acceptable, and WNUT-17 compression is a boundary case that doesn't affect core claims.

For standing with R1 on framing: R1 already found the two-layer structure informative at 3.5, while R2 wants fundamental reframing at 2.5. But attempting a fundamental reframing in 10 days risks breaking what R1 already accepted, so the safer path is to strengthen the current framing rather than overhaul it.

The strategy here is substantive presentation improvements—better grouping in Table 3, clearer measures, reframing C1 as a finding—that signal responsiveness to R2's concerns without destabilizing R1's acceptance. The real concessions already made (C1 downgrade, ε→continuous, title softening) are genuine responses to R2, not cosmetic fixes, which makes this defensible even if the AC weights R2 heavily.

I'm also flagging a few technical issues: two vLLM processes but only one registered experiment, a discrepancy between the strategy note and what's actually running with the temperature parameter, and outdated provenance labels for C010/C019 that still reference constrained-decoding.

且结论一致：\n- exp_008_re_n16_v2 (seed=42): SJ ρ=0.281, zero-F1=44.3%\n- exp_008_re_seed123 (seed=123): SJ ρ=0.284, zero-F1=44.1%\n\n两种子模式高度收敛——RE 信号弱不是 seed 噪声，是 task 本身的特性。核心 claims (C1 gap, C2 QE) 的证据全部来自 NER（3-seed bootstrap + 2 datasets + 2 models），RE 仅服务于 Claim 3 (task-complexity boundary) 的诊断角色。\n\n只要满足：(1) 主文每次引 RE 数字都带 \"preliminary / single benchmark\" 标注，(2) freeform ablation 的 RE flip (+1.69pp) 不在主文作为 claim 出现（仅 Appendix 观察），审稿人没有充分理由因此给 critical。\n\n---\n\n### Q2: 页数压缩是否会丢失关键信息？\n\n**[severity: warning] Discussion ablation（free-form ablation）移入 Appendix 是最大风险。**\n\nFree-form ablation 是 paper_framing Story Spine Layer 2(c) 的唯一受控证据——\"proven by elimination, the strongest form of experimental evidence for a negative result\"。完整移入 Appendix 后：\n\n- 主文 \"universal gap\" claim 失去受控实验支撑，退化为纯观察性结论\n- 标题改成 \"Struggle to Select\" 暗示 universality，但主文内没有 universality 的证据\n- R1 给 3.5 的一个重要理由可能正是 ablation 的 falsification 设计——如果移走，R1 可能降分\n\n**建议**：主文保留 free-form ablation **精简版**（~0.5 页：1 段设计 + 关键数字 std ratio 0.97 / tied Δ=-2pp / parse rate 99.95% + 1 句结论），完整 per-signal breakdown + RE flip 讨论放 Appendix。这样 ≤8 页可行且核心证据留在主文。\n\n**[severity: info] Table 2 N=16 + WNUT-17 移入 Appendix** — 可接受。N-scaling (exp-001) 仅覆盖 200/529 instances 且已标注 \"needs full-set confirmation\"。WNUT 是边界条件证据，非核心叙事。\n\n---\n\n### Q3: 站 R1 是否合理？R2 的根本重构要求是否有道理？\n\n**[severity: info] 站 R1 可防御，但需确保对 R2 的 concessions 足够实质性。**\n\nR2 的核心 concern（tautological gap）有部分道理——4/5 信号是 instance-level aggregates → 无法 rank candidates 这确实是定义性质。但 paper 的真正 contribution 不在这里，而在 Layer 2：LP（唯一 per-sample 信号）因 score compression 失败 + ablation 证明 compression 是 intrinsic。\n\n当前策略的 concessions 是实质性的，不是敷衍：\n- C1 从 contribution 降级为 finding（承认定义性质不是发现）\n- Table 3 分组明确标注哪些是 definitional / 哪些是 empirical\n- ε→连续指标（回应统计方法论质疑）\n- 标题软化（\"Struggle\" 替代 \"Do Not\"）\n\n这些改动足以向 R2 证明 concerns 被认真对待。**根本重构（10 天内重写核心叙事）风险极大**——可能破坏 R1 已接受的两层结构，引入新的不一致。\n\n风险点：如果 AC 完全站 R2，presentation-only 可能不够。但这是概率判断，站 R1 的期望收益更高。\n\n---\n\n### 额外发现\n\n**[severity: warning] cuda:1 有未注册 vLLM 进程。** 当前 GPU 状态：\n- cuda:0: 92%, 30444 MiB — PID 196214 VLLM::EngineCore（对应 exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07）\n- cuda:1: 92%, 30246 MiB — PID 197287 VLLM::EngineCore（**无对应 registry 条目**）\n- cuda:2/3: 空闲\n\nRegistry 仅有一个 RUNNING 实验。cuda:1 上的 vLLM 进程无注册记录。GPU 在项目白名单内（gpu_ids=[0,1,2,3]），不是越界，但违反实验可追溯性要求——需确认是否为 Worker 启动的未注册推理任务。\n\n**[severity: info] 策略与实际矛盾：** 策略说 \"R7-W1 LP T=0.7 不补跑\"，但 exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07（LLaMA CoNLL T=0.7）正在运行。D087 决策明确说\"利用空闲 GPU 补跑\"。请确认：(a) 新策略是否覆盖 D087 决定停跑？(b) 还是\"不补跑\"指不再追加 T=0.7 实验、但已启动的继续完成？如果是后者，exp_017 完成后的数据应纳入 tab:robustness。",

}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have data for two things to review:

**1. Epsilon sweep analysis (worker output)**

The epsilon sweep CSV results:
| ε | tied% | n (non-tied) | Δ(pp) | p | LP F1 | greedy F1 |
|---|-------|-------------|-------|---|-------|-----------|
| 0.01 | 1.1% | 523 | +0.58 | 0.21 | 65.01 | 64.43 |
| 0.02 | 13.6% | 457 | +0.83 | 0.15 | 64.04 | 63.21 |


| 0.03 | 31.8% | 361 | +1.31 | 0.08 | 63.54 | 62.23 |
| 0.05 | 60.3% | 210 | +2.86 | 0.004** | 62.46 | 59.60 |
| 0.10 | 83.9% | 85 | +3.69 | 0.034* | 61.48 | 57.79 |
| 0.15 | 93.2% | 36 | +4.15 | 0.11 |

As epsilon increases, the proportion of tied predictions grows substantially while the sample size shrinks, and the performance gap between the LP and greedy approaches widens—though statistical significance drops off at the higher epsilon values. 65.59 | 61.44 |
| 0.20 | 96.4% | 19 | +4.59 | 0.13 | 71.08 | 66.49 |

The code is clean and uses the right source data with proper filtering and N=8 verification. The results match the paper's framing around 60% at ε=0.05 with a +2.9pp improvement. There's a p-value difference—the paper reports p=0.01 but the sweep shows p=0.004, which actually indicates stronger significance than initially reported. The monotonic pattern holds as expected, with peak significance around ε=0.05-0.10, and this epsilon value isn't cherry-picked since the sample size is still reasonable and the effect is meaningful. Smaller epsilons don't compress enough to create the gap, while larger ones leave too few instances for statistical power. The sweep data is ready for the appendix.

Now looking at the LLaMA CoNLL results with temperature 0.7. After filtering, I have 2756 instances with a greedy F1 of 0.9266 versus oracle F1 of 0.9407, giving about 1.4 percentage points of headroom. The correlation metrics show SJ and FK performing similarly in both full and conditional settings, while LP is notably lower.

Comparing across temperature settings, the 0.7 temperature produces lower correlations than 1.0 across all signals—SJ drops from 0.431 to 0.393, FK from 0.428 to 0.388, and LP from 0.307 to 0.295. The zero-F1 rate is quite low at 4.1%, which makes sense since lower temperature reduces sampling noise.

The oracle headroom also shrinks with lower temperature (0.018 down to 0.014), reflecting less diversity to work with. Interestingly, LP's AUROC stays nearly flat at 0.751 versus 0.761, suggesting its binary discrimination is stable even as rank correlation dips slightly. This points to LP compression being somewhat intrinsic to the approach rather than just an artifact of temperature settings. The cross-model consistency checks out — LLaMA mirrors Qwen's patterns on CoNLL, temperature stability holds at T=0.7 with LP AUROC dropping only slightly from 0.761 to 0.751, and SJ continues to marginally outperform FK. But I'm noticing the report is missing EM and voting_conf metrics that appeared in other experiments, which means the inference pipeline here only computed three signals instead of five — this creates a gap for populating Table 2 if it needs all five metrics.

Now I need to figure out what the worker notification actually covered. The worker name mentions "selection_f1_table2" but the epsilon sweep lives in a different directory, so I'm trying to determine whether this was specifically about computing selection F1 metrics or if the worker handled multiple analyses.
```

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — worker_analysis_selection_f1_table2 + exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07\n\n### 1. Epsilon Sweep 分析审查\n\n**[severity: info] 代码质量和数据来源均正确。**\n\n`analysis_round8/epsilon_sweep.py` 使用 exp_012_rerun_1024 数据（统一 1024-token 管道），正确过滤 gold-empty、验证 N=8、bootstrap N=10000 iterations (seed=42)。\n\nSweep 结果确认 paper_framing 中的数字：\n- ε=0.05: tied=60.3% (paper 说 60%) ✓, Δ=+2.86pp (paper 说 +2.9pp) ✓\n- 单调趋势：ε↑ → tied↑ → Δ↑，ε=0.05 是统计功效最佳点 (n=210, p=0.004)\n- ε=0.05 非 cherry-pick：更小 ε 效应不显著（n 大但 Δ 小），更大 ε n 太少失去显著性\n\n**[severity: warning] p 值不一致需要对齐。** Paper_framing 报告 non-tied subset p=0.01，sweep 显示 p=0.004 (N_BOOT=10000)。差异方向是保守的（paper 报告了更大的 p），但修订时应统一——建议用 sweep 的 p=0.004（更大 bootstrap 样本更可靠），或在 paper 中写 p<0.005。\n\n**产出可用性**: epsilon_sweep.csv 可直接转为 Appendix 表格，支撑\"ε 不是任意阈值\"的论证。\n\n---\n\n### 2. exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 (T=0.7) 审查\n\n实验已完成（cuda:0 已释放），但 registry 状态仍为 RUNNING，需要 Agent 更新。\n\n**核心结果**（LLaMA, CoNLL, N=8, T=0.7, n=2756）:\n| 指标 | T=0.7 | T=1.0 (exp_017_llama_conll_infer) | Δ |\n|------|-------|------|---|\n| SJ ρ(full) | 0.393 | 0.431 | -0.038 |\n| FK ρ(full) | 0.388 | 0.428 | -0.040 |\n| LP ρ(full) | 0.295 | 0.307 | -0.012 |\n| LP AUROC | 0.751 | 0.761 | -0.010 |\n| Oracle headroom | 1.4pp | 1.8pp | -0.4pp |\n| Zero-F1% | 4.1% | ~4% | ≈0 |\n\n**[severity: info] 结果符合预期，支撑温度鲁棒性 claim。** T=0.7 所有信号 ρ 略降，LP AUROC 基本不变（0.751 vs 0.761, Δ<1pp）。信号排序一致：SJ > FK > LP。与 exp_005 (Qwen SciERC) 的温度趋势一致。\n\n**[severity: warning] 报告缺少 EM 和 voting_conf 指标。** 该实验 report.json 只有 SJ/FK/LP 三个信号，没有 EM（exact match）和 voting_conf（entity voting）。如果 Table 2 / tab:robustness 需要 5 信号在 T=0.7 的数据，Agent 需要对这组 samples.jsonl 运行补充分析管道。\n\n**[severity: info] GPU 资源核查通过。** cuda:0 已释放（0%/2MiB），cuda:1 正在跑 exp_lp_scierc_t07（83%/30GiB），cuda:2/3 空闲。实验使用 CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0，在项目配置允许范围内。\n\n---\n\n### 3. exp_lp_scierc_t07 资源核查\n\n**[severity: warning] Registry 中存在重复条目。** `exp_lp_scierc_t07`（ALLOCATED）和 `exp_lp_t07_scierc`（PENDING）看起来是同一实验的重复注册。`exp_lp_scierc_t07` 已实际提交到 cuda:1 并在运行，`exp_lp_t07_scierc` 应标记为 superseded/killed 避免混淆。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the results for exp_017_llama_conll_n8_t07 (LLaMA CoNLL N=8 T=0.7). Let me analyze:

**Key metrics:**
- n=2756 (gold-filtered from 3453)
- greedy_f1: 0.9266
- oracle_f1: 0.9407 (headroom: 1.4pp — very small, consistent with CoNLL being easy)
- SJ ρ full: 0.3934


- FK ρ full: 0.3879
- SJ ρ conditional: 0.4021
- FK ρ conditional: 0.3956
- LP ρ full: 0.2951
- LP ρ conditional: 0.3135
- LP AUROC full: 0.7509
- LP AUROC conditional: 0.7967
- structural_beats_surface: true (SJ > FK by Δ=0.006)

Now comparing this against the T=1.0 baseline to see how temperature affects the uncertainty estimates.

At T=0.7, all the correlation metrics are noticeably lower than at T=1.0 — SJ drops by 0.038, FK by 0.040, and LP by 0.012. The AUROC also decreases slightly, and the oracle headroom shrinks from 1.8pp to 1.4pp, suggesting the model has less room to improve at the lower temperature.

What's striking is that LP's correlation drops much less than the structural signals, so the gap between them narrows at T=0.7, yet structural signals still maintain a clear advantage with SJ at 0.393 versus LP at 0.295. The LP paradox continues to hold — despite the low correlation, AUROC stays reasonably high at 0.751, confirming that LP is still good at binary classification but struggles with ranking.

This validates the paper's main findings even at lower temperature: structural signals outperform LP on correlation, and the LP paradox persists, though the performance gap between the two signal types does compress slightly when diversity is reduced.

Now checking GPU status — cuda:0 and cuda:1 are idle while cuda:2 and cuda:3 are heavily loaded with the RE multi-seed experiments running. But I'm noticing something odd: the strategy explicitly said not to run RE multi-seed experiments, yet they're clearly executing right now. This is a direct contradiction I need to investigate.
```
